Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Preoccupation_with_Appearance\n- Answer only what the doctor asks.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I'm really struggling with how my face looks, especially my skin and my nose. I feel like they look completely deformed and ugly, even though my family keeps telling me there's nothing wrong. It's just constantly on my mind and making me miserable.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your concerns about your appearance are interfering with your daily life and activities more than feelings of sadness or hopelessness?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to compare the two, to be honest. I haven't really thought about it that way, but I just know that my appearance is what constantly occupies my mind and stops me from doing things.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'stops me from doing things' - is it that you avoid social situations or activities because of how you feel about your appearance, or are there other things that you've stopped doing as well?\n\nPatient: Yes, I avoid going out or seeing friends as much as possible because I'm so ashamed of how my face looks. I spend hours trying to cover up my skin with makeup and hats, and if I feel like I can't hide my flaws, I just stay home and cancel my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much time you spend each day thinking about your appearance or engaging in behaviors like applying makeup or checking your reflection, and has this been getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I easily spend at least three or four hours every day obsessing over my face, constantly checking my reflection in any shiny surface and meticulously putting on makeup to hide my flaws. It has definitely been getting progressively worse, taking up more and more of my day and making it almost impossible to focus on anything else.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you see a distorted version of yourself that you believe is an accurate reflection of how you actually look, or do you recognize that your perception of your appearance might be exaggerated or not entirely realistic?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. To me, the flaws feel completely real and deeply distressing, even if I can't explain why or if it seems exaggerated to other people. It just feels like that is exactly what I look like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your concerns about your appearance might be related to a problem with how you perceive yourself, rather than an actual physical flaw, or do you strongly believe that the flaws you see are real and others just don't see them?\n\nPatient: I strongly believe the flaws are completely real and highly visible. It is incredibly frustrating when everyone, including doctors, tells me they don't see anything or that I look normal. They are right there on my face, and I don't understand how other people can just ignore them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking cosmetic surgery or other medical procedures to correct the flaws you see, and if so, do you think that would finally make you feel comfortable with your appearance?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I have actually gone to several consultations and even had a few dermatological procedures to try and fix my skin and nose. I desperately hoped they would finally make me feel comfortable, but I always end up feeling unsatisfied or immediately noticing another flaw that needs to be corrected.

Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Preoccupation_with_Appearance\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\nAnswer strategy: Acknowledge the frustration of past procedures not working, but insist that the problem is indeed physical. Express hope that the right doctor or procedure could fix it, showing a lack of insight typical of the condition, while remaining focused on the desire to correct the perceived flaws."
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I'm really struggling with how my face looks, especially my skin and my nose. I feel like they look completely deformed and ugly, even though my family keeps telling me there's nothing wrong. It's just constantly on my mind and making me miserable.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your concerns about your appearance are interfering with your daily life and activities more than feelings of sadness or hopelessness?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to compare the two, to be honest. I haven't really thought about it that way, but I just know that my appearance is what constantly occupies my mind and stops me from doing things.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'stops me from doing things' - is it that you avoid social situations or activities because of how you feel about your appearance, or are there other things that you've stopped doing as well?\n\nPatient: Yes, I avoid going out or seeing friends as much as possible because I'm so ashamed of how my face looks. I spend hours trying to cover up my skin with makeup and hats, and if I feel like I can't hide my flaws, I just stay home and cancel my plans.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much time you spend each day thinking about your appearance or engaging in behaviors like applying makeup or checking your reflection, and has this been getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I easily spend at least three or four hours every day obsessing over my face, constantly checking my reflection in any shiny surface and meticulously putting on makeup to hide my flaws. It has definitely been getting progressively worse, taking up more and more of my day and making it almost impossible to focus on anything else.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you see a distorted version of yourself that you believe is an accurate reflection of how you actually look, or do you recognize that your perception of your appearance might be exaggerated or not entirely realistic?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. To me, the flaws feel completely real and deeply distressing, even if I can't explain why or if it seems exaggerated to other people. It just feels like that is exactly what I look like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your concerns about your appearance might be related to a problem with how you perceive yourself, rather than an actual physical flaw, or do you strongly believe that the flaws you see are real and others just don't see them?\n\nPatient: I strongly believe the flaws are completely real and highly visible. It is incredibly frustrating when everyone, including doctors, tells me they don't see anything or that I look normal. They are right there on my face, and I don't understand how other people can just ignore them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking cosmetic surgery or other medical procedures to correct the flaws you see, and if so, do you think that would finally make you feel comfortable with your appearance?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have actually gone to several consultations and even had a few dermatological procedures to try and fix my skin and nose. I desperately hoped they would finally make me feel comfortable, but I always end up feeling unsatisfied or immediately noticing another flaw that needs to be corrected.\n\nDoctor: It sounds like the procedures didn't provide the relief you were hoping for. Do you think that if you could find the right procedure or doctor, you would finally be able to feel satisfied with your appearance, or are you starting to consider that your concerns might not be entirely about the physical aspects of your appearance?"
  }

It is incredibly frustrating that the treatments I've had so far haven't worked, but I am absolutely certain the problem is entirely physical. I truly believe that if I can just find the right specialist or the correct procedure, they will finally be able to fix these flaws and I can be happy.
